</pre>

information and communication technology eurocall &#x27; 99 ( http : / / eurocall99 . univ-fcomte . fr ) first call for papers ( deadline : 31 january 1999 ) besancon ( france ) 15 - 18 september 1999 * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * * the yearly conference of the european association eurocall ( http : / / www . hull . ac . uk / cti / eurocall . htm ) will be held in besancon from wednesday 15th to saturday 18th september 1999 . this conference is a unique opportunity to encourage and disseminate state of the art research and applications for the use of computer mediated environments for language learning . i ) themes - - - - - - - main theme - - - - - - - - - - information and communication technology ( ict ) in varied language learning environments for parallel sessions and poster sessions , priority will be given to papers focussing on the contribution of ict to one of the following learning environments : - continuing education ; life - long learning / education . - vocational training . - open and distance learning . - language classrooms at primary , secondary and tertiary levels . - multimedia language laboratories ; language resource centres . - self - directed learning ; autonomous learning . the specific contribution of ict in one of these learning situations could be studied from the following points of view : development and implementation of hypermedia and multimedia systems ; the use of ict for providing a learning context ; the nature , the status and the role of those involved in the use of ict and their training ( teachers , trainers , facilitators , counsellors , tutors , etc . ) ; learner-centred approaches ; the role and place of ict within the larger framework of cultural , intercultural acquisition , and communicative or conceptual approaches . second theme - - - - - - - - - - - evaluation of language skills and language testing this theme will provide a framework for the theoretical and practical discussion of the contribution of ict to the evaluation of language skills from multiple approaches such as the enunciative / functional point of view to be found in the dcl and delf ( diplme elmentaire de langue francaise ) exams , or the points of view of language testing and adaptive testing . reports about ongoing european or national evaluation projects will be most welcome . these two main themes will underpin the whole conference , will be echoed in several pre-conference seminars and parallel sessions and will be taken up in keynote addresses . other themes - - - - - - - - - - - as well as the main and secondary conference themes described above , papers and presentations are invited , either at theoretical or practical levels , discussing innovations in the field of ict with a view to improving language learning and acquisition ; the relations between ict and learning models ; learner-system interaction or interaction between the agents ( learner , trainer , tutor , etc . ) through ict ; the integration of ict in training systems . the following list is merely intended as a guide and is not exhaustive : * computer mediated communication : theoretical models and sociological aspects of language learning on the internet ; virtual classrooms ; linguistic interactions ; dialog exchange systems ; e-mail exchanges ( tandems ) ; video-conferencing ; multi-modal exchanges ( audio , video ) ; moos ; chat systems ; whiteboards . * nlp ( natural language processing ) and language learning : corpus-based learning ; speech technology and computer assisted pronunciation and intonation ; electronic dictionaries for learners ; on-line spelling and style checkers ; help systems for writing , grammar-learning and error diagnosis . * hypermedia and multimedia systems : hypertext tools ; help systems for web browsing and editing ; study of the relations between the verbal and non-verbal in ict with a view to facilitating learning ; authoring systems ; evaluation of ict . * a learner model : towards a model of the learner in ict and language acquisition ; identification , influence and evaluation of learning strategies , cognitive styles and social factors . * didactics : didactic strategies and models ; which metalanguage in ict ? the role of culture . * teacher training : national and european policies in teacher training and the training of teachers in ict ; the new role ( s ) of teachers . organisation - - - - - - - - - - - the pre-conference day ( wednesday 15 sept . , 1999 ) will offer both workshops and seminars . the format of the seminars will be either that of a formal lesson / tutorial about one of the ict themes mentioned above , or that of exchanges on one particular theme focussing either on a synthesis of existing research or a discussion of new research paradigms . the conference itself ( from thursday 16 to saturday 18 ( am ) ) will offer papers given by guest speakers , parallel paper sessions , show and tell sessions , posters and an ongoing demo - fair . for more information about these activities , please look at our website . if you want to : - submit a proposal for a paper , poster or show and tell session , please use the form of the next message . proposals in english , french or any other european languages are welcome . - propose to run a workshop or a seminar , have a look at our guidelines for submissions on our website . key dates - - - - - - - - - 31 january 1999 : deadline for submitting paper , show and tell , poster abstracts . - 31 march 1999 : notification of panels &#x27; decision . - 31 may 1999 : deadline for early bird registration fee . - 16 september 1999 : abstracts publication . - 31 october 1999 : deadline for submitting full papers . - mai 2000 : publication in recall or alsic journals . contacts - - - - - - - eurocall &#x27; all material copyright ( c ) bell globemedia publishing inc . and its licensors . all rights reserved . &quot; dynegy inc . &#x27; s proposed $ 8 . 85 - billion ( u . s . ) takeover of enron corp . faces major uncertainties , ranging from possible opposition from regulators to potentially huge new liabilities and writeoffs at enron . the problem with regulators could come from antitrust worries about folding houston - based enron , the biggest u . s . trader of natural gas and electricity , into dynegy , another major energy trader , also based in houston . enron currently handles a quarter of all the natural gas and electricity traded in the united states . the combined company - - which would be called dynegy inc . - - would have 22 , 000 megawatts of generating capacity and 40 , 000 kilometres of natural gas pipelines , making it one of the largest companies in the united states in each of those categories . while enron and dynegy officials said they expect the merger to garner the necessary government approvals , the review by regulators is expected to take months . as for unpleasant financial surprises , enron has already produced a devastating stream of them over the past month , ranging from huge writeoffs of assets and reductions of shareholder equity to major downward restatements of past earnings reports . much of this turmoil has been caused by enron &#x27; s dealings with private partnerships run by its own officers . indeed , recent enron disclosures indicate that as much as half of the company &#x27; s pretax earnings in recent years came from deals with these officer - related partnerships , raising further questions about the quality of enron &#x27; s earnings and the potential for hundreds of millions of dollars of additional writeoffs or restatements . enron &#x27; s partnership dealings have sparked more than a dozen shareholder lawsuits and a formal investigation by the u . s . securities and exchange commission . in a measure of the continuing concerns over enron &#x27; s financial condition , both moody &#x27; s investors service and standard &amp; poor &#x27; s once again downgraded enron &#x27; s debt on friday , this time to just one level above non - investment grade , or &quot; junk &quot; status . they also kept the company under review for possible additional downgrades , and s &amp; p put dynegy on the same status . a downgrade to junk status could force enron to come up with hundreds of millions of dollars in cash or stock to buttress some of its complicated financial transactions . in an interview friday , dynegy chairman chuck watson said his company made &quot; a good assessment &quot; of enron &#x27; s condition and that in &quot; the worst - case we can come up with . . . we feel comfortable [ that ] the value exceeds the liabilities . &quot; mr . watson said dynegy has given itself the opportunity to back out of the proposed merger under certain conditions , which he declined to specify . a person familiar with the deal said that dynegy has a right to walk away if enron &#x27; s additional legal and financial liabilities exceed $ 3 . 5 - billion . a dynegy spokesman confirmed the figure . the price being paid - - 0 . 2685 of a dynegy share for each enron share - - is a fire - sale deal by any recent historical standard . the transaction valued enron shares at $ 10 . 41 each , based on dynegy &#x27; s price of $ 38 . 76 friday , up $ 2 . 26 in new york stock exchange trading . enron shares rose 22 cents at $ 8 . 63 in big board trading , a quarter of their price just a month ago and less than a tenth of the all - time high of nearly $ 90 a share reached last year . the deal envisions quickly pumping $ 1 . 5 - billion of cash into enron to bolster its liquidity and comfort enron &#x27; s trading partners who are increasingly nervous about continuing their business . the funds will come from chevrontexaco corp . , dynegy &#x27; s biggest shareholder with a 26 - per - cent stake . dynegy will use the money to purchase the preferred stock and other rights in an enron pipeline subsidiary . copyright ? 2000 dow jones &amp; company , inc . all rights reserved . companies it must also renegotiate contracts guaranteeing performance and warranties that have fallen into abeyance with the closure of the plant . ( c ) copyright financial times ltd . all rights reserved . http : / / www . ft . com . copyright ? 2000 dow jones &amp; company , inc . all rights reserved . watson puts trust in the quiet approach . by sheila mcnulty in houston . 11 / 12 / 2001 financial times ( c ) 2001 financial times limited . all rights reserved charles watson set out 16 years ago with &quot; five guys and a smile &quot; to build what would become dynegy . that same year , kenneth lay laid the foundations of enron by merging the assets of two established companies . he had an inter - state and intra - state natural gas pipeline company with 37 , 000 miles of pipe . enron widened its lead over the years , so that last year it reported $ 101 bn in revenue , to dynegy &#x27; s $ 29 bn . it was the number - one seller of natural gas and power in north america and operated the biggest web - based transaction system in the world . but on friday , the dynegy chief executive brought his company out from enron &#x27; s shadow , agreeing to buy its embattled rival in a deal worth about $ 9 bn in stock . overnight , dynegy became the biggest energy trader in the us . how did he do it ? where enron emphasised individuality , dynegy stressed teamwork . where enron encouraged entrepreneurship , dynegy stressed customer satisfaction . where enron urged aggression to pervade everything from its trading floors to its financial accountings , dynegy made friendliness and conservatism its culture . enron had its name emblazoned across houston &#x27; s professional baseball stadium and was building an office tower in downtown houston . dynegy was renting 28 floors out of the wells fargo complex . enron , mr watson notes , had a reputation for doing business with a swagger . &quot; if that &#x27; s your modus operandi , when things don &#x27; t go well people come after you , &quot; mr watson says . he believes that image of cockiness made questions over enron &#x27; s balance sheet spiral out of control . the balance sheet adjustment itself could not justify the massive degradation of its equity in a matter of weeks . &quot; the masses at enron are thirsting for a change in image , &quot; mr watson says . he intends to give them that . mr watson will remain in charge when dynegy takes control of enron in the coming months . he has asked mr lay to join the board , but mr lay has not made a decision . &quot; it &#x27; s been a good ride , for the most part , &quot; mr lay said friday night , but added , &quot; i would say the last three weeks have not been a lot of fun . &quot; mr watson reached out to mr lay on october 24 , as the crisis of confidence in enron became increasingly untenable . the securities and exchange commission was investigating its finances , investors were dumping its stock and the credit rating agencies were downgrading its debt . mr watson &#x27; s mother was having heart surgery and he was with her in saint louis . but he called mr lay to offer to help . in the back of his mind was the possibility of buying enron &#x27; s valuable pipeline assets to provide it with liquidity to support its fast diminishing business . two days later , mr watson went over to mr lay &#x27; s home to begin working towards a combination . in a meeting with mr lay and the rest of enron &#x27; s management team , it became clear they were &quot; willing to fix the problem whatever it took &quot; . mr watson believes the deal was put together so quickly because dynegy works with enron on a daily basis , they know each other &#x27; s people and businesses , and they are both based in houston . &quot; we &#x27; re one of the few people , maybe the only ones , who could have pulled it off , &quot; he says . now that he has done so , mr watson plans to move into enron &#x27; s new buildings , but has not decided whether to rename the stadium to reflect its new owner . his teenager wants him too . but that would seem to go against the modesty that enabled dynegy to win in the end . ( c ) copyright financial times group . http : / / www . ft . com . copyright ? 2000 dow jones &amp; company , inc . all rights reserved . ratings prove crucial at enron . by jenny wiggins in new york . 11 / 12 / 2001 financial times ( c ) 2001 financial times limited . all rights reserved credit ratings agencies consider themselves independent providers of research and information . but the role played by moody &#x27; s investors service in the sale of enron to dynegy last week shows agencies are often more than dispassionate observers . in enron &#x27; s case , its credit ratings were crucial to its survival . the company needed an investment grade credit rating to retain the confidence of its energy trading counterparties . enron &#x27; s solution was to arrange an acquisition by dynegy , but first it received the credit agencies &#x27; blessing . with the two companies close to a deal on friday morning , moody &#x27; s responded to investor concerns about the company by downgrading the company &#x27; s ratings to just one notch above junk . however , it also signalled it was prepared to sanction a deal , adding that &quot; a substantial near - term injection of equity capital &quot; would be viewed as &quot; a stabilising event &quot; . that arrived in the form of a $ 1 . 5 bn cash injection from chevrontexaco . the ratings agency was under pressure to provide a credit outlook . enron generated &quot; a tremendous amount of interest &quot; , including calls from market participants &quot; who wanted to share their opinion &quot; , said moody &#x27; s fran laserson . moody &#x27; s analyst john diaz said that while the agency had analysed enron &#x27; s financials both on and off its balance sheet , there were &quot; some issues &quot; the agency had not been aware of . this means that even as they seek to point out the risks inherent in a company &#x27; s balance sheet , they are also looking out for the long - term interest of a company and are willing to give troubled entities some time to deal with problems and get back on their feet . ( c ) copyright financial times group . http : / / www . ft . com . copyright ? 2000 dow jones section c market place dynegy &#x27; s rushed gamble on enron carries some big risks by alex berenson and richard a . oppel jr . 11 / 12 / 2001 the new york times page 1 , column 2 c . 2001 new york times company call it a $ 9 billion shotgun wedding . dynegy inc . , an energy marketer and trader based in houston , took the biggest gamble in its corporate history on friday by agreeing to buy the enron corporation , its much larger crosstown rival , for more than $ 9 billion in stock . in one decisive move , chuck watson , the chairman of dynegy , positioned his company to become the most important player in the volatile but potentially lucrative business of trading natural gas and electricity . but the hastily arranged deal carries big risks for dynegy as well , skeptical investors and wall street analysts say . no one outside enron appears to understand the company &#x27; s tangled finances fully , and the speed with which the deal was made did not give dynegy time to scrutinize in great detail enron &#x27; s trading book or the web of partnerships enron has entered to move debt off its balance sheet and hide losses . the skeptics wonder why dynegy decided to take on enron &#x27; s huge debt , which some analysts say could total $ 23 billion in loans on and off its balance sheet , instead of simply trying to hire away enron &#x27; s best traders , some of whom were already leaving the company . &#x27; &#x27; there are many risks associated with this merger that we will not know about for a while , &#x27; &#x27; said carol coale , an analyst at prudential securities . &#x27; &#x27; we &#x27; re not sure that we know everything there is to know at enron yet . &#x27; &#x27; in addition , enron &#x27; s earnings could be far lower than it has reported , said james chanos , a short - seller who has been a vocal critic . on thursday , enron said in a filing with the securities and exchange commission that it had used partnerships to overstate its earnings by a total of $ 600 million over the last five years . mr . chanos said other information in the filing indicated that more restatements were possible . with so much uncertainty surrounding enron &#x27; s finances , mr . chanos and other analysts question dynegy &#x27; s decision to move so quickly . they note that dynegy and enron often traded with each other , and some analysts had wondered whether , if enron had filed for bankruptcy , those trades might be wiped out , leaving dynegy unhedged , or unprotected against sudden movements in the prices of natural gas or electricity . if , on the other hand , enron is not forced to restate its profits down further , then dynegy has clinched an amazing bargain . analysts say enron will make $ 1 . 80 a share this year , so dynegy is paying just six times enron &#x27; s annual earnings , about a fourth the average ratio of companies in the standard &amp; poor &#x27; s 500 . dynegy said on friday that the deal should increase its per - share earnings next year by 90 to 95 cents , to $ 3 . 40 to $ 3 . 50 , even without factoring in up to a half - billion dollars in annual savings , before taxes , from the merger . dynegy &#x27; s shares closed friday at $ 38 . 76 , up $ 2 . 26 , so if the company &#x27; s forecast is accurate , it is trading at 11 times its 2002 earnings - - still a bargain compared with the average stock in the s . &amp; . p 500 . jeff dietert , an analyst with simmons &amp; company in houston , said dynegy &#x27; s estimates for its earnings in 2002 were &#x27; &#x27; conservative and very achievable . &#x27; &#x27; mr . watson said friday that dynegy was confident that enron &#x27; s operations and its trading business were healthy . &#x27; &#x27; we feel this is a very solid company with plenty of capacity to withstand whatever happens the next few months , &#x27; &#x27; he said . mr . dietert said dynegy needed to strike a deal with enron quickly to make sure that enron &#x27; s trading operations and financial health did not deteriorate any further . &#x27; &#x27; if enron preserves the value of the marketing and trading company , then there is clearly value in the enron stock , &#x27; &#x27; he said . preserving enron &#x27; s investment - grade credit rating is crucial to keeping the trading operation afloat . to help convince moody &#x27; s investors service and standard &amp; poor &#x27; s , the major rating agencies , that enron &#x27; s rating should remain investment - grade , dynegy has agreed to inject $ 1 . 5 billion into enron immediately . still , on friday , both agencies cut enron &#x27; s rating to just one notch above noninvestment grade , or junk , status , and both suggested that the rating could fall further . so investors and creditors will watch carefully this week to see whether other energy companies continue trading with enron , and whether the merger stems the outflow of talented enron traders . another question hanging over the deal is how easy it will be for dynegy to walk away if enron &#x27; s finances turn out to be worse that enron has already disclosed . the merger agreement , according to executives and investment bankers who shaped it , gives dynegy the opportunity to quit the deal without penalty if major new problems surface . but what exactly those terms are remains unknown . so far , investors are giving dynegy the benefit of the doubt . the company &#x27; s stock rose 17 percent on thursday and friday . &#x27; &#x27; the market believes chuck watson and the dynegy management team are very disciplined in the way they manage risks , &#x27; &#x27; mr . dietert said . copyright ? 2000 dow jones &amp; company , inc . all rights reserved . heard on the street basic principle of accounting tripped enron by jonathan weil staff reporter of the wall street journal 11 / 12 / 2001 the wall street journal cl ( copyright ( c ) 2001 , dow jones all six defendants have denied the sec &#x27; s allegations . andersen itself wasn &#x27; t named as a defendant and has said it believes the lawsuit against its partner was an unjustified action over questions of professional judgment . under gaap , misstatements aren &#x27; t immaterial simply because they fall beneath a numerical threshold , according to an sec accounting bulletin . under certain circumstances - - and it remains to be seen if they apply in the enron case - - the sec says intentional immaterial misstatements are unlawful . one reason is that when immaterial misstatements are combined with other misstatements , they can &quot; render the financial statements taken as a whole to be materially misleading . &quot; enron , which paid andersen $ 25 million last year in audit fees and $ 27 million for other services , is one of andersen &#x27; s biggest clients . so far , mr . tabolt , the andersen spokesman , says the sec hasn &#x27; t told andersen it is a subject of the enron probe . enron &#x27; s disavowal of its previous financial statements also is a major embarrassment for the directors who sit on the enron board &#x27; s audit committee , which serves as the overseer of enron &#x27; s financial reporting , internal controls and compliance processes . among the committee members is wendy gramm , a former chairman of the commodity futures trading commission and the wife of u . s . sen . phil gramm ( r . , texas ) . ms . gramm also was a member of the audit committee of ibp inc . , a meatpacking company that earlier this year became engulfed in an accounting debacle that prompted an sec investigation and nearly derailed tyson foods &#x27; since - completed plan to buy the company . enron last week said its audit committee had been responsible for conducting annual reviews of the transactions between enron and the partnerships run by former chief financial officer andrew s . fastow . the company said its special committee is investigating &quot; whether those controls and procedures were properly implemented . &quot; through an enron spokeswoman , ms . gramm declined to comment . copyright ? 2000 dow jones &amp; company , inc . all rights reserved . dynegy &#x27; company , inc . all rights reserved . copyright ? 2000 dow jones source : world reporter ( tm ) wessex water could become a takeover target for thames water as troubles continue to engulf its u . s . parent . the bath - based utility , which employs around 1 , 500 people , is one of several uk businesses owned by enron , which last week was forced to slice more than $ 500 million ( gbp 354 million ) from its reported profits for the past five years . enron &#x27; s much - smaller rival dynergy is set to buy the troubled giant for $ 8 billion ( gbp 5 . 6 billion ) - although the plunging value of enron has become a sticking point . enron , north america &#x27; s biggest buyer and seller of electricity and natural gas , bought wessex for gbpl . 4 billion in 1998 . at the time it said it planned to use wessex &#x27; s expertise as the foundation stone of global water company azurix . but few contracts have been secured as azurix has constantly found itself outbid by larger groups and the failure of the water business has only added to enron &#x27; s troubles . weekend speculation suggested thames water was poised to pounce on wessex as enron &#x27; s financial position gets weaker . thames water &#x27; s german utility group owner rwe is keen to expand its water interests in the uk and has considered bidding for wessex in the past . in august it was said to be running the slide rule over the firm to bolster its uk interests . it acquired thames , the neighbouring water company to wessex , last year for gbp 4 . 3 billion . a thames move for wessex would attract the attention of the industry regulator and previous similar takeover bids have been blocked . but the fact that the two are neighbours - wessex supplies an area stretching from wiltshire to somerset and dorset , while thames goes from london to swindon - could weigh in the favour of any bid , along with enron &#x27; s shaky financial position . enron last week acknowledged it overstated earnings by about 20 per cent over the past four years and kept large amounts of debt off its balance sheets through business partnerships now under investigation by the u . s . securities and exchange commission . the sec said enron &#x27; s financial statements from 1997 through the first half of 2001 &quot; should not be relied upon &quot; and that outside businesses run by enron officials during that period should have been included in the company &#x27; s earnings reports . enron &#x27; s debt ratings have also been reduced to one level above junk bond status and the company &#x27; s long - term debt ratings remain under review for further downgrade . on friday uk energy watchdog ofgem said it was monitoring how the financial crisis could impact on the uk market . as well as owning wessex , enron has three power plants on teesside whose customers include suppliers , generators and distributors . copyright ? 2000 dow jones microsoft sees reprieve setting up these private partnerships and making deals with them , 11 / 12 / 2001 business line ( the hindu ) fin . times info ltd - asia africa intel wire . business line ( the hindu ) copyright ( c ) 2001 kasturi &amp; sons ltd . all rights res &#x27; d enron is facing corruption charges , and this time it has nothing to do with maharashtra ! the shock waves began when the company announced in early october that it had a $ 618 million ( rs 2 , 966 crore ) loss in the third quarter and also disclosed write - offs to the tune of $ 1 . 2 billion ( rs 5 , 760 crore ) due to some questionable transactions . two of these deals were made public in the last couple of weeks . in one , mr andrew fastow , the company &#x27; s chief financial officer , ran a private partnership called ljm 2 co - investment lp ( named with the initials of his wife and children ) with whom enron had several financial transactions . the aim of the transactions was to hedge against fluctuating values in some of enron &#x27; s investments . mr fastow and other enron employees who were a part of this partnership made millions in fees and investment gains in their personal accounts . mr . fastow was fired after the revelations . second , the company is said to have made a $ 35 - million ( rs 168 - crore ) purchase from an entity called chewco investments lp , run by michael kopper , managing director of its north america unit . this deal is seen as an attempt by enron to park some of its debt elsewhere . enron , which began as a natural gas and energy products company , has made important forays into retail energy and bandwidth products . and its growth has been phenomenal . from $ 13 billion ( rs 62 , 400 crore ) in revenues in 1996 , the company , in 2000 , had revenues of $ 100 billion ( rs 80 , 000 crore ) . in 1999 - 2000 alone , it grew 150 per cent . as an energy trader , the company is a dominant force in the markets . it extended its trading skills and software infrastructure into the bandwidth area , where it has met with more limited success . however , another venture , named azurix , focussed on managing water supply and headed by rebecca mark ( of dabhol fame ) was a failure , leading to her exit from the company . some analysts are now beginning to wonder if the phenomenal results of the company have something to do with its creative accounting . enron is said to have borrowed large sums of money for asset purchases without the debt showing up on the company &#x27; s balance sheet . analysts who often write about enron use terms like &#x27; byzantine &#x27; and &#x27; labyrinth &#x27; in describing its financial dealings . now , they are admitting that they did not really understand what was going on . enron has been very secretive when it comes to revealing its financial arrangements . clearly , the company seems to have se - up all these private partnerships to make its balance sheet look clean and nice . in return , it allowed its senior employees to profit and fatten their wallets through the fees paid for the transactions . was it the price paid to keep quiet ? s pc manufacturers will have more freedom to ship machines with non - microsoft products and the company cannot retaliate against them ; and it will have to establish standard royalties and licensing terms . more interestingly , a three - member panel will be located at microsoft headquarters with staff and access to company records to monitor compliance with the settlement . the period is initially for five years , and is extendable to two more . while microsoft may not secure high marks for the quality of its products , the company has always been a leader with its strategy . though technology - savvy users of personal computers have always praised apple for its hardware and its operating system , microsoft is the one that has climbed to dominate with 90 per cent market share . and this is because it quickly worked with allies to establish its operating system as the more widely used standard . with comparable astuteness , the company managed to drag the anti - trust case long enough till a more sympathetic president occupied the white house . the anti - trust head under the democratic president clinton was joel klein , who was seen as a hawk , doggedly pursuing the company . so microsoft waited it out , while making significant monetary contributions to the republican party . even on the campaign trail , mr george bush made it known that he was not such a fervent devotee of anti - trust . once he came to power , the newly appointed anti - trust chief quickly moved to compromise and settle . the proposed settlement is not leaving everyone happy . the other private corporations that were affected by microsoft &#x27; s practices are clearly disappointed with the terms of the settlement . america online , now incarnated as aol time warner , is the biggest internet service provider , with about 30 million subscribers , and benefited from partnership with microsoft . but the new windows xp only has the company &#x27; s own msn service and its own messaging system , cutting out aol . sun microsystems is another affected party . sun &#x27; s java software allowed the same piece of computer code to run on many different kinds of computers . but the new windows xp has dropped support for java . thus , both sun and aol , among others , feel closed out and do not see any change in microsoft &#x27; s monopolistic behaviour . it is this fact that has also upset about six of the state governments , which are also parties to the suit . at the time of writing , they have refused to sign on . massachusetts , for one , has protested that it is an ineffectual deal . microsoft bravely moves on , surviving a possible threat of break - up , like a juggernaut crushing all that come in its path . but a recent wall street journal report has it that the controversy has taken its toll within , that employee morale is down , and several key people have left the company . even if it manages to close the chapter on its us legal troubles , the company still faces an investigation by the european commission . correction : in my column last fortnight , i incorrectly mentioned that polaroid &#x27; s fifth largest accounting firm , served as enron &#x27; s outside auditor for more than a decade , assuring investors the company &#x27; s financial statements conformed with generally accepted accounting principles . last week , the company reported that it overstated earnings by $ 586 million over four - and - a - half years , inflated shareholder equity by $ 1 . 2 billion because of an ` ` accounting error , &#x27; &#x27; and failed to consolidate results of three affiliated partnerships into its balance sheet . enron restated its financial reports as the company suffered a cash crisis triggered by disclosure of the cut in shareholder equity and the start of an sec investigation . on friday , enron , the largest energy trader , agreed to be acquired by dynegy inc . for $ 23 billion in stock and assumed debt . ` ` i &#x27; d be very surprised if the sec didn &#x27; t go after arthur andersen , &#x27; &#x27; said alan bromberg , securities law professor at southern methodist university and author of a six - volume treatise on securities fraud . ` ` i think they &#x27; ll have a hard time ignoring this one . &#x27; &#x27; andersen partner david tabolt has said the firm is cooperating with a special committee of enron &#x27; s board of directors appointed to investigate the accounting problems . ` ` issues have surfaced that have caused the company to restate its financial statements and advise investors that they should not rely on its financial statements or our audit reports , &#x27; &#x27; tabolt said , adding , ` ` we certainly recognize the appropriateness of that decision . &#x27; &#x27; settled suits the sec and shareholders can sue an accounting company for issuing false and misleading financial reports about a publicly traded company . andersen this year paid $ 130 million to settle lawsuits alleging the firm allowed waste management inc . and sunbeam corp . to file false financial reports that cost investors billions of dollars in losses . lynn turner , who was the sec &#x27; s chief accountant for three years until he resigned in august , said enron and andersen ignored a basic accounting rule when they overstated shareholder &#x27; s equity . explaining the equity reduction last week , enron said it had given common stock to companies created by enron &#x27; s former chief financial officer in exchange for notes receivable , and then improperly increased shareholder equity on its balance sheet by the value of the notes . &#x27; basic accounting &#x27; ` ` what we teach in college is that you don &#x27; t record equity until you get cash for it , and a note is not cash , &#x27; &#x27; said turner , who is now director of the center for quality financial reporting at colorado state university . ` ` it &#x27; s a mystery how both the company would violate , and the auditors would miss , such a basic accounting rule , when the number is one billion dollars . &#x27; &#x27; enron paid andersen $ 52 million last year , $ 25 million for auditing and $ 27 million for services including business and tax consulting , due diligence procedures and help in preparing sec filings , according the company &#x27; s $ 1 billion asking price for dabhol . neither man would comment on whether dynegy plans to cut jobs after the merger , or what will happen to the naming rights to enron field . the major league baseball stadium is home to the houston astros , and enron paid $ 100 million to have its name on the ballpark . ` ` the name of the company is now dynegy , &#x27; &#x27; watson said . dynegy &#x27; s acquisition requires approval from u . s . securities , antitrust and energy regulators . watson expects soon to share details of the new dynegy with regulators from the european union and u . k . , where enron operates a trading desk and owns a water company and two power plants . ` ` i don &#x27; t know if we are required to do so , but we will talk to them anyway , &#x27; &#x27; watson said . ` ` we don &#x27; t think there are going to be any problems , &#x27; &#x27; lay said . citigroup , j . p . morgan mull $ 500 mln enron stake , dow says 2001 - 11 - 12 03 : 06 ( new york ) london , nov . 12 ( bloomberg ) - - j . p . morgan chase &amp; co . and citigroup inc . may invest $ 250 million each in enron corp . , the company they are advising in its agreed takeover by dynegy inc . , to stop credit downgrades , dow jones newswires reported , citing people familiar with the matter . dynegy agreed friday to buy the largest energy trader for at least $ 23 billion , including at least $ 8 billion in stock and $ 15 billion in assumed debt . moody &#x27; s investors service maintained an investment grade rating on enron after the two banks told the credit agency they were mulling a $ 500 million investment , bolstering the company &#x27; s financial outlook , the paper said . moody &#x27; s decision removed the threat that a junk rating would create a cash crunch by forcing enron to repay early $ 3 . 3 billion of bonds . last month , j . p . morgan and citigroup gave enron a $ 1 billion credit line , using the company &#x27; s pipeline systems as collateral , the paper said . the investment , which raises conflict of interest issues , may take several weeks to complete , the paper said . citibank and j . p . morgan spokesmen declined to comment . moody &#x27; s spokesman also wouldn &#x27; t comment . ( dow jones newswires 11 - 12 ) enron corp . raised to ` hold &#x27; at prudential 2001 - 11 - 12 07 : 26 ( new york ) princeton , new jersey , nov . 12 ( bloomberg data ) - - enron corp . ( ene us ) was raised to ` ` hold &#x27; &#x27; from ` ` sell &#x27; &#x27; by analyst carol coale at prudential securities . the target price is $ 10 per share . hedging bets on the enron - dynegy deal by james j . cramer &gt; realmoney . com 11 / 12 / 2001 07 : 25 am est this enron ( ene : nyse - news - commentary ) deal is anything but definitive . in fact , it has so many loopholes in it that one has to believe that dynegy ( dyn : nyse - news - commentary ) may be going through the whole thing just to learn from the master - - or to learn what not to do , depending on your ethics . i can &#x27; t recall a deal with this many breakup provisions : pipelines going here , money going there , money from chevron ( cvx : nyse - news - commentary ) , not dynegy , caps on liabilities . to me it seems to be a bit of a charade . i don &#x27; t think , personally , there is anything at enron besides one pipeline that will generate a couple of hundred million in cash and lots of liabilities , some known , others not . the idea that enron &#x27; s traders and its trading desk are an asset is under heavy assault . why would you want to buy something that may have made no money ? on top of all of that is the possibility of massive antitrust action brought by consumers for what may have been a scheme to keep electricity off the california market . go ahead , short dynegy and go long enron . just make sure you have calls on dynegy and puts on enron to put . you &#x27; ll need them . random musings : see you on cnbc &#x27; s &quot; squawk box . &quot;
</pre>

## Record 009025

**Label:** `benign`

<pre>
URL: http://www.mozillazine.org/weblogs/hyatt/#85409355
Date: Not suppliedBlake blogs[1] about how mpt[2] wants Mozilla to look just like MSIE. I have to 
admit, the evidence is pretty compelling. I recall someone asking me, &quot;Do you 
really agree with mpt&#x27;s Top 10 list? He&#x27;s quoted you at the top of the list!&quot;Do I agree that those ten items mpt mentions are the top ten problems? Of 
course not. No two people will have the same top ten problems. Also keep in 
mind that mpt and I can agree that something is a problem without necessarily 
agreeing on the solution to the problem. Maybe we have different ideas 
regarding how to solve a particular issue, but we at least both believe it is 
an issue that needs to be addressed. That&#x27;s something.To cover the list specifically:
- Navigator chrome structure - While I don&#x27;t necessarily agree with mpt&#x27;s 
proposed default configuration, I do agree that the chrome structure is 
painfully restrictive, and that customizable toolbars need to be implemented in 
order for us to acquire the flexibility to deal with this problem.- Speed - can&#x27;t argue with this, except to say that cutting out a lot of the 
useless UI and features from the chrome helps substantially. Reduce bloat, gain 
speed.- Text editing - if you use Chimera on the Mac, you&#x27;ll see that the textfield 
widget is easily the most painful part of the entire application. It&#x27;s buggy, 
slow, misbehaves, and doesn&#x27;t edit the way you&#x27;d expect. This is IMO Chimera&#x27;s 
top usability problem.- Message display - Yes. No argument here.- Search - Yeah, it&#x27;s a mess. Don&#x27;t know if it would be in my top ten, but it&#x27;s 
a mess.- Menu structure - This gets back to my blog about how the apps should be 
separated. The menu structure has been complicated in order to deal with 
multiple applications. A clean separation naturally simplifies the menus (e.g., 
you can eliminate the New submenu easily).- Migration - A problem, but IMO not one of the top ten facing Mozilla.- Context menus - mpt complains about two-click context menus, and yet, the OS 
default on Win32 (overwhelmingly) is to bring up a context menu on a mouse up. 
If you don&#x27;t like it, complain about Win32, but don&#x27;t cite this as a Mozilla 
usability problem when we&#x27;re following the conventions of the operating system. 
(We simply listen for the WM_CONTEXTMENU message. That message fires when Win32 
wants to fire it.)- Validation - Err, no. Not a usability problem. To the average bear, this is 
completely irrelevant.- Preferences - IMO this should be much higher on the list. Preferences are a 
tangled pathetic mess. Again, separating prefs for individual apps into unique 
dialogs would simplify things a great deal, but we should also remove nearly 
half the preferences that exist from the GUI. Mozilla is ridiculously 
overconfigurable.[1] http://www.blakeross.com/archives/2002_08_18_index.html#80418641
[2] http://mpt.phrasewise.com

</pre>

trade and free markets have proven their ability to lift whole societies out of poverty&quot; I&#x27;m not a socio-political/history buff - does anybody have some clear examples? ----- Original Message ----- From: &quot;Rohit Khare&quot; To: Sent: Friday, September 20, 2002 5:10 PM Subject: sed /s/United States/Roman Empire/g &gt; &gt; A world where some live in comfort and plenty, while half of the human &gt; &gt; race lives on less than $2 a day, is neither just nor stable. &gt; &gt; Absolutely correct. Perhaps the most fundamental thing to realize about &gt; life on Earth today. &gt; &gt; The following is a fascinating document of official Government policy &gt; that bears close reading. It is the aspirations of a wonderful nation in &gt; an imperfect world. &gt; &gt; &gt; The war on terrorism is not a clash of civilizations. It does, &gt; &gt; however, reveal the clash inside a civilization, a battle for the &gt; &gt; future of the Muslim world. This is a struggle of ideas and this is an &gt; &gt; area where America must excel. &gt; &gt; I was recently at a lecture about the surprising success of Radio Sawa, &gt; our new music-and-news channel for 15-30 year old Arabs. It&#x27;s #1 in &gt; practically every market it&#x27;s entered, nearing 90% listenership in &gt; Amman. And it&#x27;s even beginning to be trusted for news, well past BBC and &gt; taking share from every other government broadcaster. &gt; &gt; It is as hard to imagine America losing a war of ideas in the long-term &gt; as it is to imagine America making any headway at all in the short term. &gt; &gt; Many of you may disagree, but I found the document below surprisingly &gt; centrist. If you know the code, you can hear clearly partisan tones, re: &gt; ICC, Taiwan Relations Act, etc. But, still, this is as much a Democratic &gt; platform as not. Africa and AIDS take up more mindshare than I feared &gt; they might. &gt; &gt; As you read, replace &quot;United States&quot; with &quot;Roman Empire&quot; and it may make &gt; as much sense, in the long view of history. I don&#x27;t know how proud to be &gt; about that, but it is telling. Sometime I daydream that the President &gt; might sit down with the nation with Perotista flip charts and explain to &gt; our citizens the sheer vastness of our 700+ military installations &gt; overseas and what they do for us. It would be a powerful education on &gt; how engaged we are in the world around us. &gt; &gt; Heck, I&#x27;d love to see a real-time map of Federal expenditures around the &gt; globe, a softly glowing necklace of embassies, carriers, arctic research &gt; stations, hotels, golf courses, warehouses, libraries, clinics and all &gt; the rest of the influence a trillion dollars here or there can buy. &gt; &gt; Of course, this still doesn&#x27;t leave me any more comfortable with the &gt; real news in this document: the Bush Doctrine for pre-emptive strikes. &gt; I&#x27;d sooner repeal the Church amendments on covert action than permit &gt; such a principle to be loosed upon the world. &gt; &gt; Rohit &gt; &gt; ----------------------------------------------------- &gt; September 20, 2002 &gt; &gt; Full Text: Bush&#x27;s National Security Strategy &gt; &gt; Following is the full text of President Bush&#x27;s new national security &gt; strategy. The document, entitled &quot;The National Security Strategy of the &gt; United States,&quot; will soon be transmitted to Congress as a declaration of &gt; the Administration&#x27;s policy. &gt; &gt; INTRODUCTION &gt; &gt; THE great struggles of the twentieth century between liberty and &gt; totalitarianism ended with a decisive victory for the forces of &gt; freedom -- and a single sustainable model for national success: freedom, &gt; democracy, and free enterprise. In the twenty-first century, only &gt; nations that share a commitment to protecting basic human rights and &gt; guaranteeing political and economic freedom will be able to unleash the &gt; potential of their people and assure their future prosperity. People &gt; everywhere want to say what they think; choose who will govern them; &gt; worship as they please; educate their children -- male and female; own &gt; property; and enjoy the benefits of their labor. These values of freedom &gt; are right and true for every person, in every society -- and the duty of &gt; protecting these values against their enemies is the common calling of &gt; freedom-loving people across the globe and across the ages. &gt; &gt; Today, the United States enjoys a position of unparalleled military &gt; strength and great economic and political influence. In keeping with our &gt; heritage and principles, we do not use our strength to press for &gt; unilateral advantage. We seek instead to create a balance of power that &gt; favors human freedom: conditions in which all nations and all societies &gt; can choose for themselves the rewards and challenges of political and &gt; economic liberty. By making the world safer, we allow the people of the &gt; world to make their own lives better. We will defend this just peace &gt; against threats from terrorists and tyrants. We will preserve the peace &gt; by building good relations among the great powers. We will extend the &gt; peace by encouraging free and open societies on every continent. &gt; &gt; Defending our Nation against its enemies is the first and fundamental &gt; commitment of the Federal Government. Today, that task has changed &gt; dramatically. Enemies in the past needed great armies and great &gt; industrial capabilities to endanger America. Now, shadowy networks of &gt; individuals can bring great chaos and suffering to our shores for less &gt; than it costs to purchase a single tank. Terrorists are organized to &gt; penetrate open societies and to turn the power of modern technologies &gt; against us. &gt; &gt; To defeat this threat we must make use of every tool in our arsenal -- &gt; from better homeland defenses and law enforcement to intelligence and &gt; cutting off terrorist financing. The war against terrorists of global &gt; reach is a global enterprise of uncertain duration. America will help &gt; nations that need our assistance in combating terror. And America will &gt; hold to account nations that are compromised by terror -- because the &gt; allies of terror are the enemies of civilization. The United States and &gt; countries cooperating with us must not allow the terrorists to develop &gt; new home bases. Together, we will seek to deny them sanctuary at every &gt; turn. &gt; &gt; The gravest danger our Nation faces lies at the crossroads of radicalism &gt; and technology. Our enemies have openly declared that they are seeking &gt; weapons of mass destruction, and evidence indicates that they are doing &gt; so with determination. The United States will not allow these efforts to &gt; succeed. We will build defenses against ballistic missiles and other &gt; means of delivery. We will cooperate with other nations to deny, &gt; contain, and curtail our enemies&#x27; efforts to acquire dangerous &gt; technologies. And, as a matter of common sense and self-defense, America &gt; will act against such emerging threats before they are fully formed. We &gt; cannot defend America and our friends by hoping for the best. So we must &gt; be prepared to defeat our enemies&#x27; plans, using the best intelligence &gt; and proceeding with deliberation. History will judge harshly those who &gt; saw this coming danger but failed to act. In the new world we have &gt; entered, the only path to safety is the path of action. &gt; &gt; As we defend the peace, we will also take advantage of an historic &gt; opportunity to preserve the peace. Today, the international community &gt; has the best chance since the rise of the nation-state in the &gt; seventeenth century to build a world where great powers compete in peace &gt; instead of continually prepare for war. Today, the world&#x27;s great powers &gt; find ourselves on the same side -- united by common dangers of terrorist &gt; violence and chaos. The United States will build on these common &gt; interests to promote global security. We are also increasingly united by &gt; common values. Russia is in the midst of a hopeful transition, reaching &gt; for its democratic future and a partner in the war on terror. Chinese &gt; leaders are discovering that economic freedom is the only source of &gt; national wealth. In time, they will find that social and political &gt; freedom is the only source of national greatness. America will encourage &gt; the advancement of democracy and economic openness in both nations, &gt; because these are the best foundations for domestic stability and &gt; international order. We will strongly resist aggression from other great &gt; powers -- even as we welcome their peaceful pursuit of prosperity, &gt; trade, and cultural advancement. &gt; &gt; Finally, the United States will use this moment of opportunity to extend &gt; the benefits of freedom across the globe. We will actively work to bring &gt; the hope of democracy, development, free markets, and free trade to &gt; every corner of the world. The events of September 11, 2001, taught us &gt; that weak states, like Afghanistan, can pose as great a danger to our &gt; national interests as strong states. Poverty does not make poor people &gt; into terrorists and murderers. Yet poverty, weak institutions, and &gt; corruption can make weak states vulnerable to terrorist networks and &gt; drug cartels within their borders. &gt; &gt; The United States will stand beside any nation determined to build a &gt; better future by seeking the rewards of liberty for its people. Free &gt; trade and free markets have proven their ability to lift whole societies &gt; out of poverty -- so the United States will work with individual &gt; nations, entire regions, and the entire global trading community to &gt; build a world that trades in freedom and therefore grows in prosperity. &gt; The United States will deliver greater development assistance through &gt; the New Millennium Challenge Account to nations that govern justly, &gt; invest in their people, and encourage economic freedom. We will also &gt; continue to lead the world in efforts to reduce the terrible toll of &gt; AIDS and other infectious diseases. &gt; &gt; In building a balance of power that favors freedom, the United States is &gt; guided by the conviction that all nations have important &gt; responsibilities. Nations that enjoy freedom must actively fight terror. &gt; Nations that depend on international stability must help prevent the &gt; spread of weapons of mass destruction. Nations that seek international &gt; aid must govern themselves wisely, so that aid is well spent. For &gt; freedom to thrive, accountability must be expected and required. &gt; &gt; We are also guided by the conviction that no nation can build a safer, &gt; better world alone. Alliances and multilateral institutions can multiply &gt; the strength of freedom-loving nations. The United States is committed &gt; to lasting institutions like the United Nations, the World Trade &gt; Organization, the Organization of American States, and NATO as well as &gt; other long-standing alliances. Coalitions of the willing can augment &gt; these permanent institutions. In all cases, international obligations &gt; are to be taken seriously. They are not to be undertaken symbolically to &gt; rally support for an ideal without furthering its attainment. &gt; &gt; Freedom is the non-negotiable demand of human dignity; the birthright of &gt; every person -- in every civilization. Throughout history, freedom has &gt; been threatened by war and terror; it has been challenged by the &gt; clashing wills of powerful states and the evil designs of tyrants; and &gt; it has been tested by widespread poverty and disease. Today, humanity &gt; holds in its hands the opportunity to further freedom&#x27;s triumph over all &gt; these foes. The United States welcomes our responsibility to lead in &gt; this great mission. &gt; &gt; I. Overview of America&#x27;s International Strategy &gt; &gt; &gt; &quot;Our Nation&#x27;s cause has always been larger than our Nation&#x27;s defense. We &gt; fight, as we always fight, for a just peace -- a peace that favors &gt; liberty. We will defend the peace against the threats from terrorists &gt; and tyrants. We will preserve the peace by building good relations among &gt; the great powers. And we will extend the peace by encouraging free and &gt; open societies on every continent.&quot; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; President Bush &gt; West Point, New York &gt; June 1, 2002 &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; The United States possesses unprecedented -- and unequaled -- strength &gt; and influence in the world. Sustained by faith in the principles of &gt; liberty, and the value of a free society, this position comes with &gt; unparalleled responsibilities, obligations, and opportunity. The great &gt; strength of this nation must be used to promote a balance of power that &gt; favors freedom. &gt; &gt; For most of the twentieth century, the world was divided by a great &gt; struggle over ideas: destructive totalitarian visions versus freedom and &gt; equality. &gt; &gt; That great struggle is over. The militant visions of class, nation, and &gt; race which promised utopia and delivered misery have been defeated and &gt; discredited. America is now threatened less by conquering states than we &gt; are by failing ones. We are menaced less by fleets and armies than by &gt; catastrophic technologies in the hands of the embittered few. We must &gt; defeat these threats to our Nation, allies, and friends. &gt; &gt; This is also a time of opportunity for America. We will work to &gt; translate this moment of influence into decades of peace, prosperity, &gt; and liberty. The U.S. national security strategy will be based on a &gt; distinctly American internationalism that reflects the union of our &gt; values and our national interests. The aim of this strategy is to help &gt; make the world not just safer but better. Our goals on the path to &gt; progress are clear: political and economic freedom, peaceful relations &gt; with other states, and respect for human dignity. &gt; &gt; And this path is not America&#x27;s alone. It is open to all. &gt; &gt; To achieve these goals, the United States will: &gt; &gt; * champion aspirations for human dignity; &gt; &gt; * strengthen alliances to defeat global terrorism and work to prevent &gt; attacks against us and our friends; &gt; &gt; * work with others to defuse regional conflicts; &gt; &gt; * prevent our enemies from threatening us, our allies, and our &gt; friends, with weapons of mass destruction; &gt; &gt; * ignite a new era of global economic growth through free markets and &gt; free trade; &gt; &gt; * expand the circle of development by opening societies and building &gt; the infrastructure of democracy; &gt; &gt; * develop agendas for cooperative action with other main centers of &gt; global power; and &gt; &gt; * transform America&#x27;s national security institutions to meet the &gt; challenges and opportunities of the twenty-first century. &gt; &gt; &gt; II. Champion Aspirations for Human Dignity &gt; &gt; &gt; &quot;Some worry that it is somehow undiplomatic or impolite to speak the &gt; language of right and wrong. I disagree. Different circumstances require &gt; different methods, but not different moralities.&quot; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; President Bush &gt; West Point, New York &gt; June 1, 2002 &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; In pursuit of our goals, our first imperative is to clarify what we &gt; stand for: the United States must defend liberty and justice because &gt; these principles are right and true for all people everywhere. No nation &gt; owns these aspirations, and no nation is exempt from them. Fathers and &gt; mothers in all societies want their children to be educated and to live &gt; free from poverty and violence. No people on earth yearn to be &gt; oppressed, aspire to servitude, or eagerly await the midnight knock of &gt; the secret police. &gt; &gt; America must stand firmly for the nonnegotiable demands of human &gt; dignity: the rule of law; limits on the absolute power of the state; &gt; free speech; freedom of worship; equal justice; respect for women; &gt; religious and ethnic tolerance; and respect for private property. &gt; &gt; These demands can be met in many ways. America&#x27;s constitution has served &gt; us well. Many other nations, with different histories and cultures, &gt; facing different circumstances, have successfully incorporated these &gt; core principles into their own systems of governance. History has not &gt; been kind to those nations which ignored or flouted the rights and &gt; aspirations of their people. &gt; &gt; Our own history is a long struggle to live up to our ideals. But even in &gt; our worst moments, the principles enshrined in the Declaration of &gt; Independence were there to guide us. As a result, America is not just a &gt; stronger, but is a freer and more just society. &gt; &gt; Today, these ideals are a lifeline to lonely defenders of liberty. And &gt; when openings arrive, we can encourage change -- as we did in central &gt; and eastern Europe between 1989 and 1991, or in Belgrade in 2000. When &gt; we see democratic processes take hold among our friends in Taiwan or in &gt; the Republic of Korea, and see elected leaders replace generals in Latin &gt; America and Africa, we see examples of how authoritarian systems can &gt; evolve, marrying local history and traditions with the principles we all &gt; cherish. &gt; &gt; Embodying lessons from our past and using the opportunity we have today, &gt; the national security strategy of the United States must start from &gt; these core beliefs and look outward for possibilities to expand liberty. &gt; &gt; Our principles will guide our government&#x27;s decisions about international &gt; cooperation, the character of our foreign assistance, and the allocation &gt; of resources. They will guide our actions and our words in international &gt; bodies. &gt; &gt; We will: &gt; &gt; * speak out honestly about violations of the nonnegotiable demands of &gt; human dignity using our voice and vote in international institutions to &gt; advance freedom; &gt; &gt; * use our foreign aid to promote freedom and support those who &gt; struggle non-violently for it, ensuring that nations moving toward &gt; democracy are rewarded for the steps they take; &gt; &gt; * make freedom and the development of democratic institutions key &gt; themes in our bilateral relations, seeking solidarity and cooperation &gt; from other democracies while we press governments that deny human rights &gt; to move toward a better future; and &gt; &gt; * take special efforts to promote freedom of religion and conscience &gt; and defend it from encroachment by repressive governments. &gt; &gt; &gt; We will champion the cause of human dignity and oppose those who resist &gt; it. &gt; &gt; III. Strengthen Alliances to Defeat Global Terrorism and Work to Prevent &gt; Attacks Against Us and Our Friends &gt; &gt; &gt; &quot;Just three days removed from these events, Americans do not yet have &gt; the distance of history. But our responsibility to history is already &gt; clear: to answer these attacks and rid the world of evil. War has been &gt; waged against us by stealth and deceit and murder. This nation is &gt; peaceful, but fierce when stirred to anger. The conflict was begun on &gt; the timing and terms of others. It will end in a way, and at an hour, of &gt; our choosing.&quot; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; President Bush &gt; Washington, D.C. (The National Cathedral) &gt; September 14, 2001 &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; The United States of America is fighting a war against terrorists of &gt; global reach. The enemy is not a single political regime or person or &gt; religion or ideology. The enemy is terrorism -- premeditated, &gt; politically motivated violence perpetrated against innocents. &gt; &gt; In many regions, legitimate grievances prevent the emergence of a &gt; lasting peace. Such grievances deserve to be, and must be, addressed &gt; within a political process. But no cause justifies terror. The United &gt; States will make no concessions to terrorist demands and strike no deals &gt; with them. We make no distinction between terrorists and those who &gt; knowingly harbor or provide aid to them. &gt; &gt; The struggle against global terrorism is different from any other war in &gt; our history. It will be fought on many fronts against a particularly &gt; elusive enemy over an extended period of time. Progress will come &gt; through the persistent accumulation of successes -- some seen, some &gt; unseen. &gt; &gt; Today our enemies have seen the results of what civilized nations can, &gt; and will, do against regimes that harbor, support, and use terrorism to &gt; achieve their political goals. Afghanistan has been liberated; coalition &gt; forces continue to hunt down the Taliban and al-Qaida. But it is not &gt; only this battlefield on which we will engage terrorists. Thousands of &gt; trained terrorists remain at large with cells in North America, South &gt; America, Europe, Africa, the Middle East, and across Asia. &gt; &gt; Our priority will be first to disrupt and destroy terrorist &gt; organizations of global reach and attack their leadership; command, &gt; control, and communications; material support; and finances. This will &gt; have a disabling effect upon the terrorists&#x27; ability to plan and operate. &gt; &gt; We will continue to encourage our regional partners to take up a &gt; coordinated effort that isolates the terrorists. Once the regional &gt; campaign localizes the threat to a particular state, we will help ensure &gt; the state has the military, law enforcement, political, and financial &gt; tools necessary to finish the task. &gt; &gt; The United States will continue to work with our allies to disrupt the &gt; financing of terrorism. We will identify and block the sources of &gt; funding for terrorism, freeze the assets of terrorists and those who &gt; support them, deny terrorists access to the international financial &gt; system, protect legitimate charities from being abused by terrorists, &gt; and prevent the movement of terrorists&#x27; assets through alternative &gt; financial networks. &gt; &gt; However, this campaign need not be sequential to be effective, the &gt; cumulative effect across all regions will help achieve the results we &gt; seek. &gt; &gt; We will disrupt and destroy terrorist organizations by: &gt; &gt; * direct and continuous action using all the elements of national and &gt; international power. Our immediate focus will be those terrorist &gt; organizations of global reach and any terrorist or state sponsor of &gt; terrorism which attempts to gain or use weapons of mass destruction &gt; (WMD) or their precursors; &gt; &gt; * defending the United States, the American people, and our interests &gt; at home and abroad by identifying and destroying the threat before it &gt; reaches our borders. While the United States will constantly strive to &gt; enlist the support of the international community, we will not hesitate &gt; to act alone, if necessary, to exercise our right of self-defense by &gt; acting preemptively against such terrorists, to prevent them from doing &gt; harm against our people and our country; and &gt; &gt; * denying further sponsorship, support, and sanctuary to terrorists &gt; by convincing or compelling states to accept their sovereign &gt; responsibilities. &gt; &gt; &gt; We will also wage a war of ideas to win the battle against international &gt; terrorism. This includes: &gt; &gt; * using the full influence of the United States, and working closely &gt; with allies and friends, to make clear that all acts of terrorism are &gt; illegitimate so that terrorism will be viewed in the same light as &gt; slavery, piracy, or genocide: behavior that no respectable government &gt; can condone or support and all must oppose; &gt; &gt; * supporting moderate and modern government, especially in the Muslim &gt; world, to ensure that the conditions and ideologies that promote &gt; terrorism do not find fertile ground in any nation; &gt; &gt; * diminishing the underlying conditions that spawn terrorism by &gt; enlisting the international community to focus its efforts and resources &gt; on areas most at risk; and &gt; &gt; * using effective public diplomacy to promote the free flow of &gt; information and ideas to kindle the hopes and aspirations of freedom of &gt; those in societies ruled by the sponsors of global terrorism. &gt; &gt; &gt; While we recognize that our best defense is a good offense we are also &gt; strengthening America&#x27;s homeland security to protect against and deter &gt; attack. &gt; &gt; This Administration has proposed the largest government reorganization &gt; since the Truman Administration created the National Security Council &gt; and the Department of Defense. Centered on a new Department of Homeland &gt; Security and including a new unified military command and a fundamental &gt; reordering of the FBI, our comprehensive plan to secure the homeland &gt; encompasses every level of government and the cooperation of the public &gt; and the private sector. &gt; &gt; This strategy will turn adversity into opportunity. For example, &gt; emergency management systems will be better able to cope not just with &gt; terrorism but with all hazards. Our medical system will be strengthened &gt; to manage not just bioterror, but all infectious diseases and &gt; mass-casualty dangers. Our border controls will not just stop &gt; terrorists, but improve the efficient movement of legitimate traffic. &gt; &gt; While our focus is protecting America, we know that to defeat terrorism &gt; in today&#x27;s globalized world we need support from our allies and friends. &gt; Wherever possible, the United States will rely on regional organizations &gt; and state powers to meet their obligations to fight terrorism. Where &gt; governments find the fight against terrorism beyond their capacities, we &gt; will match their willpower and their resources with whatever help we and &gt; our allies can provide. &gt; &gt; As we pursue the terrorists in Afghanistan, we will continue to work &gt; with international organizations such as the United Nations, as well as &gt; non-governmental organizations, and other countries to provide the &gt; humanitarian, political, economic, and security assistance necessary to &gt; rebuild Afghanistan so that it will never again abuse its people, &gt; threaten its neighbors, and provide a haven for terrorists &gt; &gt; In the war against global terrorism, we will never forget that we are &gt; ultimately fighting for our democratic values and way of life. Freedom &gt; and fear are at war, and there will be no quick or easy end to this &gt; conflict. In leading the campaign against terrorism, we are forging new, &gt; productive international relationships and redefining existing ones in &gt; ways that meet the challenges of the twenty-first century. &gt; &gt; IV. Work with Others To Defuse Regional Conflicts &gt; &gt; &gt; &quot;We build a world of justice, or we will live in a world of coercion. &gt; The magnitude of our shared responsibilities makes our disagreements &gt; look so small.&quot; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; President Bush &gt; Berlin, Germany &gt; May 23, 2002 &gt; &gt; &gt; &gt; Concerned nations must remain actively engaged in critical regional &gt; disputes to avoid explosive escalation and minimize human suffering. In &gt; an increasingly interconnected world, regional crisis can strain our &gt; alliances, rekindle rivalries among the major powers, and create &gt; horrifying affronts to human dignity. When violence erupts and states &gt; falter, the United States will work with friends and partners to &gt; alleviate suffering and restore stability. &gt; &gt; No doctrine can anticipate every circumstance in which U.S. action -- &gt; direct or indirect -- is warranted. We have finite political, economic, &gt; and military resources to meet our global priorities. The United States &gt; will approach each case with these strategic principles in mind: &gt; &gt; * The United States should invest time and resources into building &gt; international relationships and institutions that can help manage local &gt; crises when they emerge. &gt; &gt; * The United States should be realistic about its ability to help &gt; those who are unwilling or unready to help themselves. Where and when &gt; people are ready to do their part, we will be willing to move decisively. &gt; &gt; &gt; Policies in several key regions offer some illustrations of how we will &gt; apply these principles: &gt; &gt; The Israeli-Palestinian conflict is critical because of the toll of &gt; human suffering, because of America&#x27;s close relationship with the state &gt; of Israel and key Arab states, and because of that region&#x27;s importance &gt; to other global priorities of the United States. There can be no peace &gt; for either side without freedom for both sides. America stands committed &gt; to an independent and democratic Palestine, living beside Israel in &gt; peace and security. Like all other people, Palestinians deserve a &gt; government that serves their interests, and listens to their voices, and &gt; counts their votes. The United States will continue to encourage all &gt; parties to step up to their responsibilities as we seek a just and &gt; comprehensive settlement to the conflict. &gt; &gt; The United States, the international donor community, and the World Bank &gt; stand ready to work with a reformed Palestinian government on economic &gt; development, increased humanitarian assistance and a program to &gt; establish, finance, and monitor a truly independent judiciary. If &gt; Palestinians embrace democracy, and the rule of law, confront &gt; corruption, and firmly reject terror, they can count on American support &gt; for the creation of a Palestinian state. &gt; &gt; Israel also has a large stake in the success of a democratic Palestine. &gt; Permanent occupation threatens Israel&#x27;s identity and democracy. So the &gt; United States continues to challenge Israeli leaders to take concrete &gt; steps to support the emergence of a viable, credible Palestinian state. &gt; As there is progress towards security, Israel forces need to withdraw &gt; fully to positions they held prior to September 28, 2000. And consistent &gt; with the recommendations of the Mitchell Committee, Israeli settlement &gt; activity in the occupied territories must stop. As violence subsides, &gt; freedom of movement should be restored, permitting innocent Palestinians &gt; to resume work and normal life. The United States can play a crucial &gt; role but, ultimately, lasting peace can only come when Israelis and &gt; Palestinians resolve the issues and end the conflict between them. &gt; &gt; In South Asia, the United States has also emphasized the need for India &gt; and Pakistan to resolve their disputes. This administration invested &gt; time and resources building strong bilateral relations with India and &gt; Pakistan. These strong relations then gave us leverage to play a &gt; constructive role when tensions in the region became acute. With &gt; Pakistan, our bilateral relations have been bolstered by Pakistan&#x27;s &gt; choice to join the war against terror and move toward building a more &gt; open and tolerant society. The Administration sees India&#x27;s potential to &gt; become one of the great democratic powers of the twenty-first century &gt; and has worked hard to transform our relationship accordingly. Our &gt; involvement in this regional dispute, building on earlier investments in &gt; bilateral relations, looks first to concrete steps by India and Pakistan &gt; that can help defuse military confrontation. &gt; &gt; Indonesia took courageous steps to create a working democracy and &gt; respect for the rule of law. By tolerating ethnic minorities, respecting &gt; the rule of law, and accepting open markets, Indonesia may be able to &gt; employ the engine of opportunity that has helped lift some of its &gt; neighbors out of poverty and desperation. It is the initiative by &gt; Indonesia that allows U.S. assistance to make a difference. &gt; &gt; In the Western Hemisphere we have formed flexible coalitions with &gt; countries that share our priorities, particularly Mexico, Brazil, &gt; Canada, Chile, and Colombia. Together we will promote a truly democratic &gt; hemisphere where our integration advances security, prosperity, &gt; opportunity, and hope. We will work with regional institutions, such as &gt; the Summit of the Americas process, the Organization of American States &gt; (OAS), and the Defense Ministerial of the Americas for the benefit of &gt; the entire hemisphere. &gt; &gt; Parts of Latin America confront regional conflict, especially arising &gt; from the violence of drug cartels and their accomplices. This conflict &gt; and unrestrained narcotics trafficking could imperil the health and &gt; security of the United States. Therefore we have developed an active &gt; strategy to help the Andean nations adjust their economies, enforce &gt; their laws, defeat terrorist organizations, and cut off the supply of &gt; drugs, while -- as important -- we work to reduce the demand for drugs &gt; in our own country. &gt; &gt; In Colombia, we recognize the link between terrorist and extremist &gt; groups that challenge the security of the state and drug trafficking &gt; activities that help finance the operations of such groups. We are &gt; working to help Colombia defend its democratic institutions and defeat &gt; illegal armed groups of both the left and right by extending effective &gt; sovereignty over the entire national territory and provide basic &gt; security to the Colombian people. &gt; &gt; In Africa, promise and opportunity sit side by side with disease, war, &gt; and desperate poverty. This threatens both a core value of the United &gt; States -- preserving human dignity -- and our strategic priority -- &gt; combating global terror. American interests and American principles, &gt; therefore, lead in the same direction: we will work with others for an &gt; African continent that lives in liberty, peace, and growing prosperity. &gt; Together with our European allies, we must help strengthen Africa&#x27;s &gt; fragile states, help build indigenous capability to secure porous &gt; borders, and help build up the law enforcement and intelligence &gt; infrastructure to deny havens for terrorists. &gt; &gt; An ever more lethal environment exists in Africa as local civil wars &gt; spread beyond borders to create regional war zones. Forming coalitions &gt; of the willing and cooperative security arrangements are key to &gt; confronting these emerging transnational threats. &gt; &gt; Africa&#x27;s great size and diversity requires a security strategy that &gt; focuses bilateral engagement, and builds coalitions of the willing.
